Well, Minister of State, out of service! If you are not even able to design a dog in such a way that it does not eat a sausage, you could also say with regard to the budget or the precise budget truth: "As the Lord, so gscher." I would like to use this general debate today to invite you to a debate on equal opportunities between city and country, on the fair financial partnership between local authorities, i.e. on financial compensation. Actually, ladies and gentlemen, everyone should be interested in this issue, because each of us lives in a community. It would be necessary for the economy, the workers who are interested in agriculture, because it is important to have a secure financial power in all the communities of our country. The lack of financial resources in the country also threatens jobs there. The lack of jobs in rural areas strengthens commuting problems, because the inhabitants then have to commute to the workplaces – with the disadvantages of daily traffic congestion, the pre-programmed traffic infarction, so that in the city we have to build the passage around again! Do you think that's useful? In my view, it would be better to ensure a fair distribution of financial power, so that rural areas are strengthened. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We do not need a preference in this area – absolutely not! – but we believe that it requires justice, that every citizen is equal in value and that the federal income shares are actually divided according to the per capita income. Ladies and gentlemen! There will nevertheless be a different financial force, because it is a difference whether it is a large, low-inhabitant community or a pulsating tourism community, whether it is a community that has generously designed the trans-local loads such as water protection and clean areas, so that water supply can be provided in dense areas, and I recognise that water supply can be ensured, a community in which nature reserves, nature parks, or "Natura 2000" areas which, in order to meet the EU's requirements, are transmitted over regions – with all the difficulties – or whether there is a community in which there are nearby recreation areas. That is why we are always talking about the need to save the communities! If I read that the Brau Union chief, Mr. Markus Liebl, meant that the communities would also have to manage sparingly, I can only escape this: You don't need to teach that to smaller communities, because they have to practice it every day! It is obvious that the blind are talking about the color, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I will now show you this by means of two examples, at the municipality of Freistadt and the municipality of Grieskirchen – both district cities. Freistadt has 7 800 inhabitants, Grieskirchen 5 400 inhabitants. Now it is often thought that the drink tax would represent only three percent of the total budget and therefore this amount would be saved, neglected or compensated: Ladies and gentlemen! In recent years, the municipality of Freistadt has had on average between 13 and 15 million Schillings per year freely available for investment, i.e. for the extraordinary household. If the beverage tax on alcoholic beverages were not actually compensated, 50 percent of the investment budget would fall away – 50 percent! – because this drink tax amount in the municipality of Freistadt is 7 million schilling! In Grieskirchen, the loss of this drinking tax would mean about 30 percent loss, and in many small communities this would be 100 percent of the free budget, the investment budget. In addition, there would be an intensification: In the case of still so good national and federal funding of various projects, each municipality is obliged to make a certain own contribution. If it has nothing available on investment budgets, it cannot raise its own share either. Therefore, compensation is necessary, and I am glad that there is a careful and reasonable negotiation and no rapid shooting. It is important that a just and secure solution comes, ladies and gentlemen. The second point is the graded population key: Today, the financial compensation is running out. It is always argued in favour of the larger cities, because the cost of infrastructure is said to be higher. The fact is that in rural areas infrastructure costs are unevenly higher and that in road construction about triple, in water supply about four times and in wastewater disposal on average, the triple – but partly up to ten times! – to pay for it! – Read more in the ÖROK document. I urge the local government to ensure, as an equal partner, that in the forthcoming financial settlement negotiations, the graduated population key is finally more justly divided – a demand that we have been making for a long time! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] One thing has been said: A consolidated federal budget must also be the basis for the future in this area. We very much welcome this budget from the Austrian People's Party. We will prove in the coming negotiations that it is indeed a budget for the future and not like that of Edlinger one for the past!

ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00017_d7e16899	Mr President. High house! Education is a very important area. In the European Union, during the Portuguese Presidency, education will be adopted as the focus of all efforts – education, science and research, which will further develop the economy as well as give people the opportunity to accept the challenges. I think that's the right way to lead a government. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In the course of the negotiations, I have succeeded in achieving that the entire staffing effort for teachers and for university professors and professors is covered for all staff at universities in the budget. Previously, Mr Brosz said that it was impossible to vote on what had been calculated, and I would constantly go over my budget. I can therefore explain to him the facts: The Ministry's budget is based on the number of posts. The number of posts is divided by the number of lessons to be held by students by the teaching obligation. And these hours are to be kept, so these posts are to be budgeted. In the Federal Schools we have precisely calculated this, budgeted it precisely, and in the Federal Schools I have kept the budget every year. In the field of national teachers, it is different, since, in the negotiations on financial compensation between the Minister of Finance and the individual Länder, the procedures under which payments are made are determined. These arrangements then apply for five years, and the countries are calling this money. It is not possible to control these requirements within a current budget year. Although it is possible for the Court of Auditors to control the country's conduct two or three years later, another three or four years later, if something has not been voted, the Federal Government could ask for something back, only this is such a laborious procedure, so that it cannot lead to a current budget control. I have communicated this to the Minister of Finance in writing every year. But it hasn't changed anything about it. I also reported every year that the budgeting of the national teachers is not right. I have mentioned the same thing during this year's budget negotiations, and that is why, from the draft budget to the budget decision, we have also made an increase of 600 million schilling in the personnel costs of the national teachers. It is now necessary to ensure, during the forthcoming negotiations on financial compensation, that there is a ongoing controlling, ongoing control of the posts compared with the authorised posts and requirements compared to the approved posts. In this respect, a number of changes have to be made, which unfortunately has not been changed in the previous legislative period. It is therefore not true that this budget has been constantly overburdened, but it is true that it has not been properly budgeted, which I have always pointed out. Ladies and gentlemen! Education, science and research have also been recognized as so important in the creation of the budget in Austria that: Within teaching, teaching, personnel costs, we have to cover the budget fully. We also need to cover the operating costs in full. Schools receive the same amounts, the same autonomous budget as last year. The universities had a 5-percent reduction last year, this year we will reduce the operating costs by 3 percent. We have set priorities. If you ask, How can you do that with a somewhat shorter budget?, then I have to say: You just have to focus. For in the short time when this budget has been drawn up, it is not possible to put in place structural measures, it is not possible to consider where there are reasonable synergies where a colleague can take a building course together. All these considerations were not possible for the time being. In the whole school area, considerations we are now working on can only become effective from autumn 2001, because now the school year 2000/2001 is planned. All measures taken to achieve synergies must be implemented in the long term. Therefore, no structural measures could be taken in the 2000 budget, so the reduction in discretionary expenditure had to be made. In the case of discretionary expenditure, savings must be made, i.e. 15 percent, both in the field of education and in the field of science. It is not true that the expenditure on science has been reduced by one billion schillings and that the expenditure on the field of education has not been reduced. Savings have been made everywhere – in an area of 1.3 billion schilling, in the other area 1.4 billion schilling, so that our share of savings in discretionary expenditure is about 3 billion schilling. The question now is, of course, how to achieve such a reduction in discretion, especially since, as you know, huge amounts of discretion expenditure are compulsory expenditure. If you have to finance the Vienna International School, this is a contractual obligation. In the same way, there are enormously many contractual obligations in the university area. Of course, those school buildings that are under construction are being rebuilt, because this is done by the BIG, a company that has been outsourced, and of course it continues to work. Plans already started will continue, but any good and beautiful improvement will not be possible this year. [[Abg. Dr. Petrovic: § 5 applies! It clearly applies § 5!]] They said that this was an circumvention of the Council of Ministers because according to § 5 para. 2 War material law in this case would have been necessary to obtain the approval of the Council of Ministers. If you do, however, use this Section 5 para. 2 read correctly, then it says here: Exports – exports! – of war material – and I now commend it: by the Federal Ministry of Defence – such approval by the Council of Ministers needs. – So if we send today, for example – which is often happening – a device for repairing a foreign company, then we need the approval of the Council of Ministers. But I believe, Mrs Petrovic, I have clearly stated that not the Federal Ministry of Defence has carried out these devices, but that the Federal Ministry of State Defense has sold these equipment to a foreign company in the country. This foreign company has sought an export permit by means of the approval procedure under § 3 of the War Material Act – that is a private company, which is why § 3 is applied here – and the Ministry of the Interior has granted it, and that is why this export has also been lawful. I would ask you to make a real distinction here: If an Austrian institution operates a business with a foreigner in Austria, this question is to be separated from whether and under what conditions this foreign acquirer can also spend this acquired property abroad. The standard purpose of this regulation, however, has nothing to do with the standard purpose of the war material law. You then referred to the Ministry of the Interior's notice, Mrs Petrovic, that these weapons would have been demilitarized. – Just, Mrs Petrovic, to make clear here too: This notice goes to the acquirer, to the private firm Brugger + Thomet, and not to the Federal Ministry of State Defense, because there is no obligation to demilitarize these weapons when sold domestically. "Demilitarisation," Mrs Petrovic said, however, does not mean to make the weapons useless, but to make them useless only for military use, i.e. to prevent the possibility of permanent shooting. Of course, it is still a functional weapon like any other sports weapon or civil weapon. – Here, too, were a few shortcomings in your argumentation. Mrs Petrovic: We can only rely on our laws, on our guidelines, and they have been observed in this case.
